<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove conformal universality of mixed bulk spin and energy correlations for the square-lattice Ising model with sufficiently small, square-symmetric, finite-range even multispin perturbations of either sign. One critical-temperature branch and two field normalizations apply in every bounded simply connected <i>C</i><sup>2</sup> Jordan domain with free, plus, or minus boundary conditions, and in the periodic thermodynamic plane state. Energies are centered in their actual states.

- **claim-conformal-universality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-model-main)): For each $J$ and potential $V$ as above, there are $\lambda_0>0$ and real functions $\beta_c,Z_\sigma,Z_\epsilon$ on $(-\lambda_0,\lambda_0)$, continuous at zero, such that $$\beta_c(\lambda)>0,\quad \beta_c(0)=\beta_0,\qquad Z_\sigma(0)=Z_\epsilon(0)=1,\quad Z_\sigma(\lambda)Z_\epsilon(\lambda)\ne0.$$ For every fixed $\|\lambda\|<\lambda_0$, every allowed domain $D$, every $b\in\{\mathrm{free},+,-\}$, all integers $n,m\ge0$ with $n+m\ge1$, all pairwise distinct interior points $x_1,\ldots,x_n,y_1,\ldots,y_m$, and all $j_1,\ldots,j_m\in\{1,2\}$, the full-sequence limit is $$\label{model:limit} \lim_{a\downarrow0} \left\langle\prod_{i=1}^n S_a(x_i) \prod_{k=1}^m E_{a,j_k}(y_k)\right\rangle_{a,D}^{\lambda,b} =Z_\sigma(\lambda)^nZ_\epsilon(\lambda)^m \mathcal C_D^b(\boldsymbol x;\boldsymbol y).$$ The same functions and coupling interval apply to every such choice.* *At each fixed mesh, the infinite-volume limit along periodic square boxes exists for all local observables. Using that state first, and then taking $a\downarrow0$, gives <a href="#model:limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:limit">[model:limit]</a> with $D$ replaced by $\mathbb C$. In particular its two-spin limit is a nonzero constant times $\|x-y\|^{-1/4}$.* *If $\phi:D\to D'$ is a conformal bijection between allowed domains, the limiting correlation in $D$ is its counterpart at the image points in $D'$, multiplied by $$\label{model:covariance} \prod_{i=1}^n\|\phi'(x_i)\|^{1/8} \prod_{k=1}^m\|\phi'(y_k)\|.$$ The boundary type is transported unchanged.
  - Explanation: For a small coupling, one temperature branch and two field factors give the scaling limit of mixed bulk spin and energy correlations, with conformal covariance, in every allowed domain and in the periodic plane state.
- **claim-pointwise-ratio** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-ratio)): Let $G$ be a finite graph with nonnegative pair couplings and arbitrary finite real fields. Let $I,J,F$ be disjoint vertex sets, with $I,J$ nonempty, and prescribe arbitrary common spin values on $F$. Form the graph obtained by deleting $F$ and contracting $I$ and $J$ separately; parallel couplings are added. Let $q_0$ be the probability that the two contracted vertices are FK-connected in the zero-field, cluster-weight $2$ measure on this graph. For any configurations $a$ on $I$ and $b,b'$ on $J$, $$\label{in:ratio-bound} e^{-4\operatorname{arctanh}q_0} \leq \frac{\mathbb P(\sigma_I=a\mid\sigma_J=b,\sigma_F)} {\mathbb P(\sigma_I=a\mid\sigma_J=b',\sigma_F)} \leq e^{4\operatorname{arctanh}q_0}.$$ Either conditional law may be replaced by a mixture of such laws. All external fields, including those produced by the common pins, are set to zero in the comparator defining $q_0$.
  - Explanation: Conditional probability ratios with common pinned spins are bounded by a zero-field FK connection probability on the graph with the pins deleted.
- **claim-buffered-patch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-patch)): Fix $c>0$. If $I\subset B_R(z)$ and $J\subset B_{(1+c)R}(z)^c$, their conditional density ratios in Lemma <a href="#in:ratio" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="in:ratio">2</a> lie in $[C_c^{-1},C_c]$, uniformly in the mesh, common pins, and fields. There are $C<\infty$ and $\theta>0$ such that, if $I\subset B_w(z)$, $J\subset B_R(z)^c$, and $R\geq8w\geq8$ in lattice units, the absolute log density ratio is at most $$\label{in:patch-bound} C(w/R)^\theta.$$ No unpinned neighborhood of $I$ is required.* *The buffered bound holds for fixed polygonal separating collars with positive relative width, and for their intersections with a free or fixed physical boundary. It also compares different continuations or volumes when the reference interaction agrees in the separating chart. The same assertions hold for mixtures and after a common conditional randomization of the local spins. Finitely many shrinking patches can be changed successively.
  - Explanation: Separated buffered sets have uniformly bounded density ratios, and shrinking patches give a power-law bound on the log density ratio.
- **claim-ordered-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-ordered)): Suppose $\mu^-\preceq\mu^+$ are two ordered laws, possibly on different volumes or with different pins. Assume there is a common unpinned source contour $S_0$ such that, given its spins, both laws have the same critical nearest-neighbor continuation toward $T$. Apart from the common pinning data, this continuation has zero field. All changed data lie on the source side of $S_0$. For an admissible intermediate cut $J$, there are $C<\infty$ and $\theta\in(0,1/8)$ such that $$\label{in:ordered-bound} \left\\|\mu_T^+-\mu_T^-\right\\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}} \leq C(w/s)^{-7/8+\theta} \bigl(\mathbb E_{\mu^+}M_J-\mathbb E_{\mu^-}M_J\bigr).$$ Here total variation is the supremum of event-probability differences. The constants depend on the fixed chart and admissibility constants, not on the source values or their number. At a fixed positive $w/s$, vanishing rescaled magnetic difference implies vanishing target total variation. Random ordered source configurations are allowed.
  - Explanation: For ordered laws with a common continuation, target total variation is controlled by the difference of cut magnetizations.
- **claim-stopping-band-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-transfer)): Work at mesh $\delta$ in a fixed square chart with homogeneous fixed sign on its outer wall. Fix nested squares $Q_u\Subset Q_v$ and a target $T$ outside $Q_v$ and inside the wall, with positive clearances from both. Reserve a slightly larger band window, also separated from $T$ and the wall. The levels $u,v$ may be chosen generically in prescribed separated subintervals of this window.* *Initially reveal $Q_u$ and its cutting contour. There is a finite adaptive spin exploration through the band, with full transcript $\mathcal F^\delta$, such that the unexamined sites, conditional on each realizable transcript, have the ordinary Gibbs law with the examined spins fixed. Given $\epsilon,\eta>0$, this observation has a descriptor $$Z^\delta\in\{0,1,\ldots,m\}$$ computed from band spins, with the following properties for all sufficiently small $\delta$:* 1. *$\mathbb P(Z^\delta=0)\leq\epsilon$ in the fixed-wall reference law.* 2. *For every $i\geq1$ there is a deterministic regular signed Jordan barrier $\Gamma_i$, with finitely many sign arcs, on the fresh side of the target-facing revealed boundary. It separates the reveal from $T$ and has a positive clearance from $T$ and the distant wall. For *every* realizable transcript with $Z^\delta=i$, adding the plus barrier pins and then the minus barrier pins changes the target law by a sum of at most $\eta$ in total variation.* 3. *After these additions, the target-side law is a single law $Q_{i,\delta}$, determined by $\Gamma_i$ and the unchanged outer wall, independent of the particular transcript in bin $i$.* *The finite list, all its positive clearances, and $m$ are chosen before the mesh threshold, depending only on the geometry and $\epsilon,\eta$. They work simultaneously for all bounded target tests, including mesh-dependent tests. For a finite list of bands, the descriptors may be chosen with converging joint laws. If the band-spin distribution under another law has density at most $C$ relative to the reference distribution, its exceptional probability is at most $C\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: A finite adaptive exploration yields a descriptor so that, per transcript, adding deterministic signed barriers reproduces the target law up to a small total variation.
- **claim-static-critical-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-fields-necklace)): The following facts concern only the critical nearest-neighbor model.* 1. * For bounded finitely connected domains without singleton boundary components and with finitely many nondegenerate fixed-plus, fixed-minus, or free boundary arcs, separated bulk spin and energy correlations converge with dimensions $1/8$ and $1$. Discrete domains converge in the Caratheodory sense, with eventually matching boundary-component and arc counts and converging boundary marks. Convergence is uniform on compact separated configurations. The limits are conformally covariant and continuous under these marked-domain limits. The energy convention initially subtracts its plane mean; expanding the finitely many subtractions gives the same assertion for domain-centered energies. The two bond directions have the same scalar limit. Homogeneous half-plane spin limits and full-plane spin limits are included, with odd plane correlations zero.* 2. * Normalize the continuum spin so that its plane two-point function is $\|z-w\|^{-1/4}$. Write $G_n$ for its $n$-point function. For even $n$, $$\label{in:plane-spin} G_{n,\mathbb C}(z_1,\ldots,z_n)^2 =2^{-n/2} \sum_{\substack{\mu\in\{-1,1\}^n\\\sum_i\mu_i=0}} \prod_{i<j}\|z_i-z_j\|^{\mu_i\mu_j/2}.$$ For $z_i$ in the upper half-plane, put $q_{ij}=\|(z_i-z_j)/(z_i-\overline z_j)\|$. Then $$\begin{aligned} G_{n,\mathbb H}^{+}(z)^2 &=2^{-n/2}\prod_i(2\operatorname{Im}z_i)^{-1/4} \sum_{\mu\in\{-1,1\}^n}\prod_{i<j}q_{ij}^{\mu_i\mu_j/2}, \label{in:halfplane-plus}\\ G_{n,\mathbb H}^{\mathrm{free}}(z)^2 &=c_n\prod_i(2\operatorname{Im}z_i)^{-1/4} \sum_{\mu\in\{-1,1\}^n} \left(\prod_i\mu_i\right) \prod_{i<j}q_{ij}^{\mu_i\mu_j/2},\quad n\ \hbox{even}. \label{in:halfplane-free} \end{aligned}$$ Here $c_n=(-1)^{n/2}2^{-n/2}$ for even $n$, including $c_0=1$. The plus formula holds also for odd $n$; minus boundary is obtained by spin flip. These functions inherit reflection positivity from the lattice and satisfy spherical covariance in the plane. The plus-disk one-point functions of the spin and the plane-centered scalar energy are nonzero.* 3. * For homogeneous plus, minus, or free reference laws and for the plane, the rescaled two-point bound is $$\delta^{-1/4}\|\mathbb E\sigma_z\sigma_{z'}\| \leq C(\|z-z'\|\vee\delta)^{-1/4}$$ on buffered compact sets. More generally, if $z_1,\ldots,z_n$ lie in a fixed compact interior set and $\rho_i$ is the lesser of a fixed buffer radius and one quarter of the distance to the other insertions, then $$\label{in:product-arm} \left\|\mathbb E\prod_i\sigma_{z_i}\right\| \leq C_n\prod_i \left(\frac{\delta}{\rho_i\vee\delta}\right)^{1/8}.$$ Repeated sites are allowed with the factor capped at one. The estimate uses ordinary FK wiring and homogeneous cluster coloring; prescribed mixed boundary signs are handled by buffered density comparison. For bounded compactly supported piecewise regular profiles $h$, the variables $X_\delta(h)=\delta^{15/8}\sum_z h(z)\sigma_z$ have uniform exponential moments of every fixed real order under homogeneous reference laws on buffered compact sets. Positive-profile bounds follow from GHS; signed profiles follow by splitting the signs and applying Cauchy–Schwarz. These moment inputs also hold in homogeneous half-plane references by local comparison.* 4. *In a fixed square with free boundary and zero field, for every $\epsilon>0$ there are $K<\infty$ and $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\delta$, with probability at least $1-\epsilon$ a nearest-neighbor vertex circuit surrounds the concentric square of one third the side length. The circuit is contained in the union of at most $K$ FK clusters, each of cardinality at least $c\delta^{-15/8}$; successive clusters may be separated by one nearest-neighbor step. For a uniform positive field $h\delta^{15/8}$, conditional on internal FK bonds, a cluster $C$ is attached to the plus ghost with probability $$\label{in:ghost} \tanh(h\delta^{15/8}\|C\|),$$ independently for distinct internal clusters. Internal bonds dominate their zero-field law. Consequently, at fixed physical square size, the probability of a plus-spin circuit tends to one by choosing a sufficiently large finite $h$ and then a sufficiently small mesh.
  - Explanation: Collects the reference critical-model inputs: primary-field convergence, explicit spin formulas, moment bounds and the necklace circuit estimate.
- **claim-collar-tilt** (theorem, [source](#stmt-buf-tilt)): Let $T$ be a separating contour and $W$ a farther contour, with a fixed positive buffer between them. Fix two outer Gibbs continuations, independently of the data on the inner side of $T$. They agree on the graph, couplings, and fields between $T$ and $W$, and all their differences are on or beyond $W$. For any fixed data on the inner side of $T$, the corresponding laws on $T$ differ by a positive tilt $w$ satisfying $$\frac{\sup w}{\inf w}\le K, \label{buf:tilt-bound}$$ where $K$ depends only on the buffer geometry. The same weight $w$ works for every change of the inner data. Common pins and arbitrary common finite fields are permitted wherever [in:ratio](#in:ratio) permits them.* *If two laws $\nu_1,\nu_2$ on $T$ are compared in one continuation, their laws in the other satisfy $$\left\\|\nu_1^w-\nu_2^w\right\\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}} \le 2K\left\\|\nu_1-\nu_2\right\\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}}, \qquad d\nu_i^w=\frac{w\,d\nu_i}{\nu_i(w)}. \label{buf:tilt-tv}$$ In the destination continuation, both tilted laws use the same further Gibbs kernel given $T$. Applying that kernel preserves the bound. Remote data may first be conditioned on, or integrated out in the outer Gibbs weights before the inner data are varied.
  - Explanation: Changing the outer continuation beyond an unchanged collar tilts the contour law by a weight of bounded oscillation, which bounds total variation transport.
- **claim-soft-barrier** (theorem, [source](#stmt-buf-barrier)): Fix a regular signed Jordan contour with finitely many sign arcs in an interior square, a buffered outer target contour, and a more distant square wall with a constant prescribed sign. Given $\eta>0$, there is a bounded piecewise regular profile $h$, supported in arbitrarily small prescribed neighborhoods of the signed contour, such that, for all sufficiently small $\delta$, the law on the target with the contour pinned and the law tilted by $U_{\delta,h}$ have total variation less than $\eta$. The contour pins are not imposed in the latter law. The profile and all its nonzero tube widths are fixed before $\delta$ tends to zero. The conclusion transfers through any unchanged outer buffer as in [buf:tilt](#buf:tilt), and can be applied successively to finitely many disjoint buffered squares.
  - Explanation: A pinned signed contour can be replaced on the target by a soft exponential field weight to any prescribed total variation accuracy.
- **claim-soft-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-buf-soft)): In the preceding reference geometries, for every $\eta>0$ there is a finite list of bounded piecewise regular profiles $h_1,\ldots,h_N$, and a fine-mesh threshold, such that every inner observable $F_\delta$ with $\left\\|F_\delta\right\\|_\infty\le1$ has coefficients $c_1(\delta),\ldots,c_N(\delta)$ satisfying $$\sum_{i=1}^N\|c_i(\delta)\|\le1, \qquad \sup_{\left\\|H_\delta\right\\|_\infty\le1} \left\|\mathbb E[F_\delta H_\delta] -\sum_{i=1}^N c_i(\delta) \mathbb E[U_{\delta,h_i}H_\delta]\right\|<\eta. \label{buf:one-side}$$ The supremum is over all outer observables, including the constant one. The profiles and threshold are independent of $F_\delta$ and $H_\delta$. The coefficients may depend on the mesh and on the observable. The analogous statement holds with the inner and outer roles reversed.* *Inner replacement profiles can be supported within distance $Cs$ of the center; outer replacement profiles can be supported where $cR\le \|z\|\le CR$. The two profile regions have a fixed relative radial separation. Each profile is supported in a compact interior set avoiding the center, with additional positive clearances that may depend on $\eta$.* *More precisely, let $(F_{\alpha,\delta})_{\alpha=1}^p$ and $(H_{\beta,\delta})_{\beta=1}^q$ be finite families of inner and outer observables, all of sup norm at most one. There are finite inner and outer profile lists $(h_i)$ and $(g_j)$ and coefficient vectors $c_{\alpha i}(\delta),d_{\beta j}(\delta)$ such that $$\begin{gathered} \sum_i\|c_{\alpha i}(\delta)\|\le1,\qquad \sum_j\|d_{\beta j}(\delta)\|\le1,\label{buf:matrix-mass}\\ \left\|\mathbb E[F_{\alpha,\delta}H_{\beta,\delta}] -\sum_{i,j}c_{\alpha i}(\delta)d_{\beta j}(\delta) \mathbb E[U_{\delta,h_i}U_{\delta,g_j}]\right\|<\eta \quad\text{for every }\alpha,\beta.\label{buf:two-side} \end{gathered}$$ The same row and column vectors work throughout this matrix. Adjoin the zero profile, for which $U_{\delta,0}=1$, to each list. A constant observable can then be retained exactly; adjoining it to either family controls all the separate means with the same coefficients. The profile lists and mesh threshold are independent of the observables. The preceding support bounds apply to both lists. Parity and plane lattice-rotation symmetries may be imposed by averaging. The same conclusions hold along the approaching-half-plane charts described above.
  - Explanation: Any bounded inner observable is replaced, against all bounded outer tests, by a finite signed combination of soft weights with coefficient mass at most one.
- **claim-smeared-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-buf-moments)): Let $h_1,\ldots,h_m$ be fixed bounded piecewise regular profiles with compact interior supports in a reference chart. All joint polynomial moments of the variables $X_{\delta,h_i}$ converge to the integrals of the corresponding continuum spin correlations. For every $t<\infty$, $$\sup_{\delta\ \mathrm{small}} \mathbb E\exp\!\left(t\sum_{i=1}^m\|X_{\delta,h_i}\|\right)<\infty. \label{buf:exponential}$$ Joint exponential expectations converge as well. In particular the weights in <a href="#buf:weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="buf:weight">[buf:weight]</a> have convergent normalizations and uniformly integrable powers of every fixed positive order.* *For two separated support regions with a fixed buffer, there is a geometric constant $K$ such that $$\mathbb E[A U_{\delta,g}]\le K\mathbb EA \label{buf:weighted-tail}$$ for every nonnegative function $A$ of the first region and every normalized positive soft weight on the second. The constant is independent of $g$, its magnitude, and its complexity.
  - Explanation: Smeared spin fields have convergent polynomial moments, uniform exponential tails, and a separated-region weighted bound.
- **claim-bulk-conditional-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rad-operator)): There are constants $C<\infty$ and $\ell_0<\infty$ with the following property. Fix $\ell=R/s\geq\ell_0$. For all sufficiently large $s$, with its threshold allowed to depend on $\ell$, let $F$ be any bounded lattice function supported in $B_s(0)$. Put $$m(F)=\mathbb E_{\mathbb C}F, \qquad d_R(F)=\mathbb E^+_{\mathrm{disk}(4R)}F-m(F).$$ Then $$\begin{aligned} \|d_R(F)\|&\leq C(s/R)^d\\|F\\|_\infty,\label{eq:rad-response}\\ \\|P_RF-m(F)\\|_\infty &\leq C\|d_R(F)\|+C(s/R)^p\\|F\\|_\infty, \label{eq:rad-operator} \end{aligned}$$ with $$(d,p)= \begin{cases} (1,3),&F\text{ even and invariant under right-angle rotations},\\ (1,2),&F\text{ even},\\ (1/8,9/8),&F\text{ odd}. \end{cases}$$ The constants are independent of every sufficiently large fixed ratio. The same conclusion holds along ratios tending to a fixed $\ell\geq\ell_0$, after harmless changes in the thresholds.
  - Explanation: Conditional averaging of a bounded local function is controlled by its disk response plus a power remainder, with exponent pairs depending on parity and rotation symmetry.
- **claim-charge-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rad-identity)): For every even list $X$, $$\mathbb E_0\left\|\sum_i\mu_i z_i\right\|^2 =4\left(\frac{W_2(X)}{W_0(X)}\right)^2. \label{eq:rad-charge-identity}$$ For the empty list both sides are zero.
  - Explanation: An exact charge identity relates the second moment of the dipole sum to the ratio of charge-two and charge-zero weights.
- **claim-low-spectrum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rad-spectrum)): Let $\Pi_0$ be projection onto the vacuum. The following statements hold on the indicated closed sectors of $\mathcal H$.* 1. *In the even plane sector there is a nonzero rank-one projection $\Pi_1$ at dimension one and $$\\|D_t-\Pi_0-t\Pi_1\\|\leq t^2.$$ On the subspace invariant under rotation by $\pi/2$, the right side may be replaced by $t^3$.* 2. *In the odd plane sector there is a nonzero rank-one projection $\Pi_{1/8}$ and $$\\|D_t-t^{1/8}\Pi_{1/8}\\|\leq t^{9/8}.$$* 3. *In the fixed half-plane, and in the even sector of the free half-plane, $$\\|D_t-\Pi_0\\|\leq t^2.$$* *The vacuum matrix element is $\langle v,\Pi_0w\rangle =\overline{\mathbb Ev}\,\mathbb Ew$.
  - Explanation: The dilation operators on the radial Hilbert space have the vacuum, the energy line and the spin line as the low spectrum in each sector, with the stated remainders.
- **claim-radial-norms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rad-soft-norm)): Consider the continuum normalized soft weights supplied by Lemma <a href="#buf:soft" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="buf:soft">9</a>. If their supports lie on the inner side of a radial circle with a fixed relative buffer, each weight defines an element of $\mathcal H$ of norm at most a constant depending only on that buffer geometry. The bound is independent of the bounded profile, its magnitude, and the accuracy used to choose the finite dictionary. A linear combination with total absolute coefficient sum at most $B$ has norm at most $CB$.
  - Explanation: Normalized soft weights define radial states whose norms are bounded independently of profile and accuracy.
- **claim-disk-calibration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rad-calibration)): Let $\mathcal O_{1/8}(z)=\sigma_z$ and let $\mathcal O_1(z)=e(z)$ be the symmetric average of the four incident bond products. With $m$ denoting infinite-plane critical expectation, there are nonzero real constants $\kappa_d$ such that $$\lim_{R\to\infty}R^d\left( \mathbb E^+_{\mathrm{disk}(4R)}\mathcal O_d(0) -m(\mathcal O_d(0))\right)=\kappa_d, \qquad d\in\{1/8,1\}. \label{eq:rad-micro-calibration}$$
  - Explanation: The microscopic spin and energy have nonzero disk responses at the expected decay rates.
- **claim-boundary-conditional-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rad-boundary)): Fix support and separation factors, including a constant $C_0$. There are $C<\infty$ and $\ell_0<\infty$ such that the following holds. For each fixed $\ell=R/s\geq\ell_0$, there are a lower threshold for $s$, a sufficiently large chart-window factor, and a sufficiently small flatness threshold, all allowed to depend on $\ell$ but not on the boundary orientation. Suppose a physical boundary point lies within $C_0s$ of the common center of the support and cutting squares, and that throughout the chart window the boundary is a single $C^1$ graph with slope deviation from its tangent below that threshold. Retain its homogeneous nearest-neighbor boundary type in the conditional averaging. Then, for every bounded $F$ supported in the inner square, $$\inf_{c\in\mathbb R}\\|P_RF-c\\|_\infty \leq C(s/R)^2\\|F\\|_\infty \label{eq:rad-boundary-operator}$$ in fixed boundary conditions. The same holds for even $F$ in free boundary conditions. For complex $F$, take $c\in\mathbb C$ and adjust the constant harmlessly.
  - Explanation: Near a nearly flat physical boundary, conditional averaging has remainder of order the squared scale ratio.
- **claim-order-of-choices** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): The order of choices in the two propositions is important. The large ratio is fixed first; approximation tolerances smaller than its spectral remainder are then fixed, and only afterward is the lattice scale required to be sufficiently large. The geometric and spectral constants are uniform over large fixed ratios, but no uniform rate in that last fine-mesh passage has been asserted. For a fixed compact $C^2$ Jordan boundary, the graph and flatness conditions of Proposition <a href="#rad:boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rad:boundary">16</a> hold on all sufficiently small physical windows, uniformly along the boundary.
  - Explanation: Remark on the order in which ratio, tolerances and lattice scale must be chosen.
- **claim-carrier-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rg-geometry)): For connected $A$ and $R=Lr$, with $L$ sufficiently large, $$N_R(U(A))\le C\bigl(1+L^{-1}N_r(A)\bigr). \label{rg:geometry-eq}$$ Moreover $A+B_{5R}$ can be covered by at most $C_L(1+N_r(A))$ squares of radius $r$. These estimates hold on a torus while $R$ is less than a fixed sufficiently small fraction of its side.
  - Explanation: Thickened connected carriers have controlled coarse volume, and a neighborhood is covered by a controlled number of squares.
- **claim-exact-analytic-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rg-map)): For each fixed sufficiently large $L$, the maps $\mathcal R_j$ are analytic on a common complex neighborhood of zero in <a href="#rg:norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:norm">[rg:norm]</a>, uniformly in $j$ and the finite volume. They satisfy $\mathcal R_j(0)=0$ and $$\\|\mathcal R_j(h)-T_jh\\|_R\le C_L\\|h\\|_r^2,\qquad \\|D\mathcal R_j(h)-T_j\\|\le C_L\\|h\\|_r. \label{rg:quadratic}$$ The derivative bound holds also from the marked norm at $r$ to the marked norm at $R$. For a marked coefficient, the corresponding scalar-increment functional is bounded, and its derivative differs from its derivative at zero by at most $C_L\\|h\\|_r$ in the same marked norms.* *The coefficient estimates remain valid if the output weight $e^{N_R(\text{output carrier})}$ is replaced by $e^{2N_R(Q)}$. In particular, restricting an output list to connected test carriers with diameter at least $D$ gives the additional factor $$e^{-cD/R}. \label{rg:diameter-gain}$$ This factor can be retained simultaneously with the norm of any small input, including a marked remainder or a background. The assertion holds before scalar projection as well as after it, and includes long unchanged singletons with their test neighborhoods.* *For any fixed number of square-free source labels, the induced maps on the corresponding coefficients are polynomial and uniformly Lipschitz on bounded sets of marked coefficients, while the background remains in this common small ball. All the preceding enhanced-weight and diameter bounds apply to these maps. Constants may depend on the number of labels and their norm bounds.* *In finite volume the transformation, including its scalar factor, preserves the partition integral exactly. The same conclusions apply to a preliminary transformation of finite-range microscopic interactions to a fixed large scale $r_0$, averaging every singleton there; its constants may also depend on $r_0$ and the finite microscopic range. Finitely many prescribed source carriers may instead be left unchanged if they are too large for these cutting squares, with constants allowed to depend on those carriers.
  - Explanation: The exact change-of-interaction map is analytic, quadratically close to its linearization, preserves the partition integral and gains an exponential diameter factor.
- **claim-distinguished-fields** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rg-fields)): On carrier $B_{r_j}(z)$ define $$v_j^{(d)}(z)=r_j^d\pi P_{r_j,z}\mathcal O_d(z), \qquad d\in\{1,1/8\}. \label{rg:distinguished}$$ For spin the projection removes no constant. If $r_0$ is sufficiently large, there are constants $0<c<C<\infty$, independent of $j$ and of the large blocking ratio $L$, such that $$c\le\\|v_j^{(d)}(z)\\|_\infty\le C. \label{rg:field-norms}$$ Their single-term marked norms are likewise bounded above and below. For $R/r_j$ sufficiently large, $$c(r_j/R)^d\le \|d_R(v_j^{(d)}(z))\| \le C(r_j/R)^d, \label{rg:field-response}$$ where the disk is centered at $z$.
  - Explanation: The normalized spin and energy fields have two-sided norm bounds and disk responses of the expected scaling.
- **claim-linear-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rg-linear)): Given $\rho>0$, choose $L$ sufficiently large and then $r_0$ sufficiently large. In the homogeneous background space there are uniformly bounded splittings into the line $\mathbb R\boldsymbol v_j$ and a complement such that $$T_j= \begin{pmatrix}L&B_j\\0&D_j\end{pmatrix},\qquad \\|D_j\\|\le\rho,\qquad \\|B_j\\|\le C_L. \label{rg:background-matrix}$$ The coordinates in this splitting are denoted by $(t_j,w_j)$.* *For a singleton marked source, in the even sector with $d=1$ or the odd sector with $d=1/8$, the scaled operator $L^dT_j$ has a corresponding splitting about $v_j^{(d)}$ with matrix $$\begin{pmatrix}1&B_j^{(d)}\\0&D_j^{(d)}\end{pmatrix},\qquad \\|D_j^{(d)}\\|\le\rho,\qquad \\|B_j^{(d)}\\|\le C_L. \label{rg:marked-matrix}$$ The even projections can be chosen invariant under square rotations.* *In a finite domain consider a boundary family: every carrier touches a fixed bounded lattice neighborhood of the physical edge. Its ordinary linear image, modulo constants, has norm at most $\rho$ times its input norm, provided the local graph and flatness conditions of [rad:boundary](#rad:boundary) hold on the required windows at the current scale. For free boundary conditions the family must be even; for either fixed boundary condition no parity restriction is imposed. The image is again a boundary family.
  - Explanation: The linear map splits into one expanding thermal line and contracting complements, and boundary families contract.
- **claim-decaying-graph** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flow-stable-graph)): There is $\epsilon>0$ such that, for every sufficiently small $w_0\in W_0$, there is exactly one trajectory of <a href="#flow:coordinate-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:coordinate-map">[flow:coordinate-map]</a> with this initial stable coordinate, $\sup_j(\|t_j\|+\left\\|w_j\right\\|_{r_j})<\epsilon$, and $$\sup_{j\ge0}\gamma_0^{-j} (\|t_j\|+\left\\|w_j\right\\|_{r_j})<\infty.$$ It satisfies $$\label{flow:stable-bound} \|t_j\|+\left\\|w_j\right\\|_{r_j}\le C\left\\|w_0\right\\|_{r_0}\gamma_0^j.$$ Its initial thermal coordinate $t_0=\psi(w_0)$ is analytic, with $\psi(0)=0$. This graph is transverse to the thermal line.
  - Explanation: Each small stable coordinate determines a unique decaying trajectory, giving an analytic graph transverse to the thermal line.
- **claim-temperature-branch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flow-branch)): There is $\lambda_0>0$ and a real analytic branch $\beta_c:(-\lambda_0,\lambda_0)\to(0,\infty)$, with $\beta_c(0)=\beta_0$, whose homogeneous effective backgrounds satisfy $$\label{flow:plane-decay} h_{j+1}=\mathcal R_j(h_j),\qquad \left\\|h_j\right\\|_{r_j}\le C\|\lambda\|\gamma_0^j.$$ The branch depends only on $J,V,\lambda$.
  - Explanation: An analytic temperature branch through the nearest-neighbor critical value yields exponentially decaying homogeneous backgrounds.
- **claim-boundary-backgrounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flow-boundary)): There are constants $C,\lambda_0>0$, independent of the allowed domain and of its boundary type, with the following property. For each fixed $C^2$ Jordan domain $D$ there is $\eta_D>0$ such that, for sufficiently small $a$, the effective background with any of the three prescribed boundary conditions has the form $$\label{flow:domain-decomposition} H_j^{a,b}=\operatorname{Res}_{a,j}h_j+b_j^{a,b}, \qquad \left\\|b_j^{a,b}\right\\|_{r_j} \le C\|\lambda\|\gamma^j,$$ through all steps with $ar_j\le\eta_D$. Every carrier of $b_j^{a,b}$ touches the physical boundary in the above sense. For free boundary conditions its functions are even; for fixed boundary conditions they need not be even.
  - Explanation: In a finite domain the background is the restricted plane background plus a boundary part that decays exponentially in the step.
- **claim-periodic-backgrounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flow-torus)): For a sufficiently small fixed $\vartheta>0$, the periodic effective background $H_j^N$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \left\\|H_j^N\right\\|_{r_j}&\le C\|\lambda\|\gamma^j, \label{flow:torus-small}\\ \left\\|H_j^N-\Pi_Nh_j\right\\|_{r_j} &\le C\|\lambda\|\gamma^j \exp(-cN/r_j), \label{flow:torus-error} \end{aligned}$$ whenever $r_j\le\vartheta N$. The constants and the coupling interval are independent of $N,j$. On every fixed finite set of scales, coefficients in a fixed plane chart converge to their plane counterparts as $N\to\infty$, with the tails interpreted in the weighted list norms.* *For real $\lambda$ these backgrounds are real. At every finite volume the successive maps, including the preliminary map, retain the exact partition identity with their scalar prefactors, and the residual tilt defines a positive Gibbs probability.
  - Explanation: Periodic backgrounds are small and differ from projected plane backgrounds by an error exponentially small in the box side over the scale.
- **claim-one-source-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-src-scalar)): There are real analytic functions $Z_\sigma(\lambda)$ and $Z_\epsilon(\lambda)$ on a neighborhood of zero, with $$Z_\sigma(0)=Z_\epsilon(0)=1,$$ such that the plane one-source lists on the critical trajectory satisfy $$u_j^\ell =Z_\ell(\lambda)v_j^{(d_\ell)}+\eta_j^\ell, \qquad \left\\|\eta_j^\ell\right\\|_{r_j,\bullet}\longrightarrow0. \label{src:one-source}$$ Here $Z_\ell$ is $Z_\sigma$ for a spin and $Z_\epsilon$ for any incident bond orientation; energy lists are understood modulo constants. The convergence is locally uniform in the coupling. The two functions are nonzero on one smaller coupling interval, independently of domains, boundary conditions, and the number of sources.
  - Explanation: One-source lists converge to analytic multiples of the distinguished fields, defining the two field normalizations.
- **claim-separated-sources** (theorem, [source](#stmt-src-separated)): At the stopping scale just described, the exact finite-system source coefficients satisfy $$\begin{aligned} U_k^{\{\ell\}} &=Z_\ell v_k^{(d_\ell)}+o(1) &&\text{in marked norm}, \label{src:single-stop}\\ \left\\|U_k^S\right\\|_{r_k,\bullet}&=o(1) &&(\|S\|\geq2), \label{src:multiple-stop}\\ c_k^S&=o(1) &&\bigl(\|S\|\geq2\text{ or }S \text{ is a spin singleton}\bigr). \label{src:scalar-stop} \end{aligned}$$ The ideal functions in <a href="#src:single-stop" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="src:single-stop">[src:single-stop]</a> are restricted to the system, with their cutting squares contained in its interior. On tori the conclusions hold as $a\to0$ and $N/\Delta_*\to\infty$. All errors use the running normalization $r_k^{d(S)}$.
  - Explanation: At the stopping scale, multi-source and scalar coefficients vanish and single sources equal their ideal fields.
- **claim-periodic-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-src-plane)): After one common decrease of the coupling interval, the following hold.* 1. *At the selected temperature, every bounded observable of finitely many lattice spins has a limit along periodic square boxes as their side tends to infinity. In particular their finite-dimensional laws have a periodic thermodynamic limit at each fixed lattice spacing.* 2. *Let $j\to\infty$ and $N/r_j\to\infty$. For each fixed $D<\infty$, the expectations of every test of sup norm at most one supported in a square of radius $D r_j$ under the residual tilted torus law $\nu_j^N$ and the critical nearest-neighbor torus law differ by $o(1)$, uniformly over these tests.
  - Explanation: Periodic box limits of local observables exist, and residual tilted laws agree locally with critical torus laws.
- **claim-reference-plane-correlations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-src-reference-plane)): For every finite configuration of pairwise distinct physical insertion points, use the spin and energy normalizations of <a href="#model:spin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:spin">[model:spin]</a>–<a href="#model:energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:energy">[model:energy]</a>, with each energy centered in its actual nearest-neighbor state. The full-plane critical mixed correlations then have full-sequence scaling limits. These limits are independent of bond directions and equal the limits of their bounded-domain counterparts along an exhaustion by fixed-plus disks, using the actual disk-state means there.
  - Explanation: Plane critical mixed correlations have full-sequence limits equal to the limit of fixed-plus disk correlations.

```json
{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-main",
        "exact_quote": "For each $J$ and potential $V$ as above, there are $\\lambda_0>0$ and real functions $\\beta_c,Z_\\sigma,Z_\\epsilon$ on $(-\\lambda_0,\\lambda_0)$, continuous at zero, such that $$\\beta_c(\\lambda)>0,\\quad \\beta_c(0)=\\beta_0,\\qquad\n Z_\\sigma(0)=Z_\\epsilon(0)=1,\\quad\n Z_\\sigma(\\lambda)Z_\\epsilon(\\lambda)\\ne0.$$ For every fixed $|\\lambda|<\\lambda_0$, every allowed domain $D$, every $b\\in\\{\\mathrm{free},+,-\\}$, all integers $n,m\\ge0$ with $n+m\\ge1$, all pairwise distinct interior points $x_1,\\ldots,x_n,y_1,\\ldots,y_m$, and all $j_1,\\ldots,j_m\\in\\{1,2\\}$, the full-sequence limit is $$\\label{model:limit}\n \\lim_{a\\downarrow0}\n \\left\\langle\\prod_{i=1}^n S_a(x_i)\n                    \\prod_{k=1}^m E_{a,j_k}(y_k)\\right\\rangle_{a,D}^{\\lambda,b}\n =Z_\\sigma(\\lambda)^nZ_\\epsilon(\\lambda)^m\n       \\mathcal C_D^b(\\boldsymbol x;\\boldsymbol y).$$ The same functions and coupling interval apply to every such choice.*\n\n*At each fixed mesh, the infinite-volume limit along periodic square boxes exists for all local observables. Using that state first, and then taking $a\\downarrow0$, gives <a href=\"#model:limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"model:limit\">[model:limit]</a> with $D$ replaced by $\\mathbb C$. In particular its two-spin limit is a nonzero constant times $|x-y|^{-1/4}$.*\n\n*If $\\phi:D\\to D'$ is a conformal bijection between allowed domains, the limiting correlation in $D$ is its counterpart at the image points in $D'$, multiplied by $$\\label{model:covariance}\n \\prod_{i=1}^n|\\phi'(x_i)|^{1/8}\n \\prod_{k=1}^m|\\phi'(y_k)|.$$ The boundary type is transported unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-in-ratio",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite graph with nonnegative pair couplings and arbitrary finite real fields. Let $I,J,F$ be disjoint vertex sets, with $I,J$ nonempty, and prescribe arbitrary common spin values on $F$. Form the graph obtained by deleting $F$ and contracting $I$ and $J$ separately; parallel couplings are added. Let $q_0$ be the probability that the two contracted vertices are FK-connected in the zero-field, cluster-weight $2$ measure on this graph. For any configurations $a$ on $I$ and $b,b'$ on $J$, $$\\label{in:ratio-bound}\n e^{-4\\operatorname{arctanh}q_0}\n \\leq\n \\frac{\\mathbb P(\\sigma_I=a\\mid\\sigma_J=b,\\sigma_F)}\n      {\\mathbb P(\\sigma_I=a\\mid\\sigma_J=b',\\sigma_F)}\n \\leq e^{4\\operatorname{arctanh}q_0}.$$ Either conditional law may be replaced by a mixture of such laws. All external fields, including those produced by the common pins, are set to zero in the comparator defining $q_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-in-patch",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $c>0$. If $I\\subset B_R(z)$ and $J\\subset B_{(1+c)R}(z)^c$, their conditional density ratios in Lemma <a href=\"#in:ratio\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"in:ratio\">2</a> lie in $[C_c^{-1},C_c]$, uniformly in the mesh, common pins, and fields. There are $C<\\infty$ and $\\theta>0$ such that, if $I\\subset B_w(z)$, $J\\subset B_R(z)^c$, and $R\\geq8w\\geq8$ in lattice units, the absolute log density ratio is at most $$\\label{in:patch-bound}\n C(w/R)^\\theta.$$ No unpinned neighborhood of $I$ is required.*\n\n*The buffered bound holds for fixed polygonal separating collars with positive relative width, and for their intersections with a free or fixed physical boundary. It also compares different continuations or volumes when the reference interaction agrees in the separating chart. The same assertions hold for mixtures and after a common conditional randomization of the local spins. Finitely many shrinking patches can be changed successively.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-in-ordered",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\mu^-\\preceq\\mu^+$ are two ordered laws, possibly on different volumes or with different pins. Assume there is a common unpinned source contour $S_0$ such that, given its spins, both laws have the same critical nearest-neighbor continuation toward $T$. Apart from the common pinning data, this continuation has zero field. All changed data lie on the source side of $S_0$. For an admissible intermediate cut $J$, there are $C<\\infty$ and $\\theta\\in(0,1/8)$ such that $$\\label{in:ordered-bound}\n \\left\\|\\mu_T^+-\\mu_T^-\\right\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\leq C(w/s)^{-7/8+\\theta}\n       \\bigl(\\mathbb E_{\\mu^+}M_J-\\mathbb E_{\\mu^-}M_J\\bigr).$$ Here total variation is the supremum of event-probability differences. The constants depend on the fixed chart and admissibility constants, not on the source values or their number. At a fixed positive $w/s$, vanishing rescaled magnetic difference implies vanishing target total variation. Random ordered source configurations are allowed.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-in-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Work at mesh $\\delta$ in a fixed square chart with homogeneous fixed sign on its outer wall. Fix nested squares $Q_u\\Subset Q_v$ and a target $T$ outside $Q_v$ and inside the wall, with positive clearances from both. Reserve a slightly larger band window, also separated from $T$ and the wall. The levels $u,v$ may be chosen generically in prescribed separated subintervals of this window.*\n\n*Initially reveal $Q_u$ and its cutting contour. There is a finite adaptive spin exploration through the band, with full transcript $\\mathcal F^\\delta$, such that the unexamined sites, conditional on each realizable transcript, have the ordinary Gibbs law with the examined spins fixed. Given $\\epsilon,\\eta>0$, this observation has a descriptor $$Z^\\delta\\in\\{0,1,\\ldots,m\\}$$ computed from band spins, with the following properties for all sufficiently small $\\delta$:*\n\n1.  *$\\mathbb P(Z^\\delta=0)\\leq\\epsilon$ in the fixed-wall reference law.*\n\n2.  *For every $i\\geq1$ there is a deterministic regular signed Jordan barrier $\\Gamma_i$, with finitely many sign arcs, on the fresh side of the target-facing revealed boundary. It separates the reveal from $T$ and has a positive clearance from $T$ and the distant wall. For *every* realizable transcript with $Z^\\delta=i$, adding the plus barrier pins and then the minus barrier pins changes the target law by a sum of at most $\\eta$ in total variation.*\n\n3.  *After these additions, the target-side law is a single law $Q_{i,\\delta}$, determined by $\\Gamma_i$ and the unchanged outer wall, independent of the particular transcript in bin $i$.*\n\n*The finite list, all its positive clearances, and $m$ are chosen before the mesh threshold, depending only on the geometry and $\\epsilon,\\eta$. They work simultaneously for all bounded target tests, including mesh-dependent tests. For a finite list of bands, the descriptors may be chosen with converging joint laws. If the band-spin distribution under another law has density at most $C$ relative to the reference distribution, its exceptional probability is at most $C\\epsilon$.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },

Given $\\epsilon,\\eta>0$, this observation has a descriptor $$Z^\\delta\\in\\{0,1,\\ldots,m\\}$$ computed from band spins, with the following properties for all sufficiently small $\\delta$:*\n\n1.  *$\\mathbb P(Z^\\delta=0)\\leq\\epsilon$ in the fixed-wall reference law.*\n\n2.  *For every $i\\geq1$ there is a deterministic regular signed Jordan barrier $\\Gamma_i$, with finitely many sign arcs, on the fresh side of the target-facing revealed boundary.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-in-fields-necklace",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"in:fields\" label=\"in:fields\"></span> The following facts concern only the critical nearest-neighbor model.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"in:fields-convergence\" label=\"in:fields-convergence\"></span> For bounded finitely connected domains without singleton boundary components and with finitely many nondegenerate fixed-plus, fixed-minus, or free boundary arcs, separated bulk spin and energy correlations converge with dimensions $1/8$ and $1$. Discrete domains converge in the Caratheodory sense, with eventually matching boundary-component and arc counts and converging boundary marks. Convergence is uniform on compact separated configurations. The limits are conformally covariant and continuous under these marked-domain limits. The energy convention initially subtracts its plane mean; expanding the finitely many subtractions gives the same assertion for domain-centered energies. The two bond directions have the same scalar limit. Homogeneous half-plane spin limits and full-plane spin limits are included, with odd plane correlations zero.*\n\n\n\n2.  *<span id=\"in:fields-formulas\" label=\"in:fields-formulas\"></span> Normalize the continuum spin so that its plane two-point function is $|z-w|^{-1/4}$. Write $G_n$ for its $n$-point function. For even $n$, $$\\label{in:plane-spin}\n     G_{n,\\mathbb C}(z_1,\\ldots,z_n)^2\n     =2^{-n/2}\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\mu\\in\\{-1,1\\}^n\\\\\\sum_i\\mu_i=0}}\n           \\prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{\\mu_i\\mu_j/2}.$$ For $z_i$ in the upper half-plane, put $q_{ij}=|(z_i-z_j)/(z_i-\\overline z_j)|$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n     G_{n,\\mathbb H}^{+}(z)^2\n     &=2^{-n/2}\\prod_i(2\\operatorname{Im}z_i)^{-1/4}\n           \\sum_{\\mu\\in\\{-1,1\\}^n}\\prod_{i<j}q_{ij}^{\\mu_i\\mu_j/2},\n           \\label{in:halfplane-plus}\\\\\n     G_{n,\\mathbb H}^{\\mathrm{free}}(z)^2\n     &=c_n\\prod_i(2\\operatorname{Im}z_i)^{-1/4}\n           \\sum_{\\mu\\in\\{-1,1\\}^n}\n              \\left(\\prod_i\\mu_i\\right)\n              \\prod_{i<j}q_{ij}^{\\mu_i\\mu_j/2},\\quad n\\ \\hbox{even}.\n           \\label{in:halfplane-free}\n    \\end{aligned}$$ Here $c_n=(-1)^{n/2}2^{-n/2}$ for even $n$, including $c_0=1$. The plus formula holds also for odd $n$; minus boundary is obtained by spin flip. These functions inherit reflection positivity from the lattice and satisfy spherical covariance in the plane. The plus-disk one-point functions of the spin and the plane-centered scalar energy are nonzero.*\n\n3.  *<span id=\"in:fields-moments\" label=\"in:fields-moments\"></span> For homogeneous plus, minus, or free reference laws and for the plane, the rescaled two-point bound is $$\\delta^{-1/4}|\\mathbb E\\sigma_z\\sigma_{z'}|\n     \\leq C(|z-z'|\\vee\\delta)^{-1/4}$$ on buffered compact sets. More generally, if $z_1,\\ldots,z_n$ lie in a fixed compact interior set and $\\rho_i$ is the lesser of a fixed buffer radius and one quarter of the distance to the other insertions, then $$\\label{in:product-arm}\n     \\left|\\mathbb E\\prod_i\\sigma_{z_i}\\right|\n     \\leq C_n\\prod_i\n           \\left(\\frac{\\delta}{\\rho_i\\vee\\delta}\\right)^{1/8}.$$ Repeated sites are allowed with the factor capped at one. The estimate uses ordinary FK wiring and homogeneous cluster coloring; prescribed mixed boundary signs are handled by buffered density comparison. For bounded compactly supported piecewise regular profiles $h$, the variables $X_\\delta(h)=\\delta^{15/8}\\sum_z h(z)\\sigma_z$ have uniform exponential moments of every fixed real order under homogeneous reference laws on buffered compact sets. Positive-profile bounds follow from GHS; signed profiles follow by splitting the signs and applying Cauchy–Schwarz. These moment inputs also hold in homogeneous half-plane references by local comparison.*\n\n4.  *In a fixed square with free boundary and zero field, for every $\\epsilon>0$ there are $K<\\infty$ and $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, with probability at least $1-\\epsilon$ a nearest-neighbor vertex circuit surrounds the concentric square of one third the side length. The circuit is contained in the union of at most $K$ FK clusters, each of cardinality at least $c\\delta^{-15/8}$; successive clusters may be separated by one nearest-neighbor step. For a uniform positive field $h\\delta^{15/8}$, conditional on internal FK bonds, a cluster $C$ is attached to the plus ghost with probability $$\\label{in:ghost}\n     \\tanh(h\\delta^{15/8}|C|),$$ independently for distinct internal clusters. Internal bonds dominate their zero-field law. Consequently, at fixed physical square size, the probability of a plus-spin circuit tends to one by choosing a sufficiently large finite $h$ and then a sufficiently small mesh.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-in-fields-necklace",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"in:fields\" label=\"in:fields\"></span> The following facts concern only the critical nearest-neighbor model.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"in:fields-convergence\" label=\"in:fields-convergence\"></span> For bounded finitely connected domains without singleton boundary components and with finitely many nondegenerate fixed-plus, fixed-minus, or free boundary arcs, separated bulk spin and energy correlations converge with dimensions $1/8$ and $1$. Discrete domains converge in the Caratheodory sense, with eventually matching boundary-component and arc counts and converging boundary marks. Convergence is uniform on compact separated configurations. The limits are conformally covariant and continuous under these marked-domain limits. The energy convention initially subtracts its plane mean; expanding the finitely many subtractions gives the same assertion for domain-centered energies. The two bond directions have the same scalar limit. Homogeneous half-plane spin limits and full-plane spin limits are included, with odd plane correlations zero.*\n\n\n\n2.  *<span id=\"in:fields-formulas\" label=\"in:fields-formulas\"></span> Normalize the continuum spin so that its plane two-point function is $|z-w|^{-1/4}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-buf-tilt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a separating contour and $W$ a farther contour, with a fixed positive buffer between them. Fix two outer Gibbs continuations, independently of the data on the inner side of $T$. They agree on the graph, couplings, and fields between $T$ and $W$, and all their differences are on or beyond $W$. For any fixed data on the inner side of $T$, the corresponding laws on $T$ differ by a positive tilt $w$ satisfying $$\\frac{\\sup w}{\\inf w}\\le K,\n \\label{buf:tilt-bound}$$ where $K$ depends only on the buffer geometry. The same weight $w$ works for every change of the inner data. Common pins and arbitrary common finite fields are permitted wherever [in:ratio](#in:ratio) permits them.*\n\n\n*If two laws $\\nu_1,\\nu_2$ on $T$ are compared in one continuation, their laws in the other satisfy $$\\left\\|\\nu_1^w-\\nu_2^w\\right\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\le 2K\\left\\|\\nu_1-\\nu_2\\right\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}},\n \\qquad\n d\\nu_i^w=\\frac{w\\,d\\nu_i}{\\nu_i(w)}.\n \\label{buf:tilt-tv}$$ In the destination continuation, both tilted laws use the same further Gibbs kernel given $T$. Applying that kernel preserves the bound. Remote data may first be conditioned on, or integrated out in the outer Gibbs weights before the inner data are varied.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-buf-tilt",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a separating contour and $W$ a farther contour, with a fixed positive buffer between them. For any fixed data on the inner side of $T$, the corresponding laws on $T$ differ by a positive tilt $w$ satisfying $$\\frac{\\sup w}{\\inf w}\\le K,\n \\label{buf:tilt-bound}$$ where $K$ depends only on the buffer geometry. The same weight $w$ works for every change of the inner data. Common pins and arbitrary common finite fields are permitted wherever [in:ratio](#in:ratio) permits them.*\n\n\n*If two laws $\\nu_1,\\nu_2$ on $T$ are compared in one continuation, their laws in the other satisfy $$\\left\\|\\nu_1^w-\\nu_2^w\\right\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\le 2K\\left\\|\\nu_1-\\nu_2\\right\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}},\n \\qquad\n d\\nu_i^w=\\frac{w\\,d\\nu_i}{\\nu_i(w)}.\n \\label{buf:tilt-tv}$$ In the destination continuation, both tilted laws use the same further Gibbs kernel given $T$. Applying that kernel preserves the bound. Remote data may first be conditioned on, or integrated out in the outer Gibbs weights before the inner data are varied.",

Applying that kernel preserves the bound. Remote data may first be conditioned on, or integrated out in the outer Gibbs weights before the inner data are varied.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-buf-barrier",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a regular signed Jordan contour with finitely many sign arcs in an interior square, a buffered outer target contour, and a more distant square wall with a constant prescribed sign. Given $\\eta>0$, there is a bounded piecewise regular profile $h$, supported in arbitrarily small prescribed neighborhoods of the signed contour, such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, the law on the target with the contour pinned and the law tilted by $U_{\\delta,h}$ have total variation less than $\\eta$. The contour pins are not imposed in the latter law. The profile and all its nonzero tube widths are fixed before $\\delta$ tends to zero. The conclusion transfers through any unchanged outer buffer as in [buf:tilt](#buf:tilt), and can be applied successively to finitely many disjoint buffered squares.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-buf-barrier",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a regular signed Jordan contour with finitely many sign arcs in an interior square, a buffered outer target contour, and a more distant square wall with a constant prescribed sign. Given $\\eta>0$, there is a bounded piecewise regular profile $h$, supported in arbitrarily small prescribed neighborhoods of the signed contour, such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, the law on the target with the contour pinned and the law tilted by $U_{\\delta,h}$ have total variation less than $\\eta$. The contour pins are not imposed in the latter law. The profile and all its nonzero tube widths are fixed before $\\delta$ tends to zero. The conclusion transfers through any unchanged outer buffer as in [buf:tilt](#buf:tilt), and can be applied successively to finitely many disjoint buffered squares.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a regular signed Jordan contour with finitely many sign arcs in an interior square, a buffered outer target contour, and a more distant square wall with a constant prescribed sign. Given $\\eta>0$, there is a bounded piecewise regular profile $h$, supported in arbitrarily small prescribed neighborhoods of the signed contour, such that, for all sufficiently small $\\delta$, the law on the target with the contour pinned and the law tilted by $U_{\\delta,h}$ have total variation less than $\\eta$. The contour pins are not imposed in the latter law. The profile and all its nonzero tube widths are fixed before $\\delta$ tends to zero. The conclusion transfers through any unchanged outer buffer as in [buf:tilt](#buf:tilt), and can be applied successively to finitely many disjoint buffered squares.",
      "explanation": "A pinned signed contour can be replaced on the target by a soft exponential field weight to any prescribed total variation accuracy.",

      "id": "claim-soft-barrier",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-buf-barrier",
          "exact_quote": "there is a bounded piecewise regular profile $h$",
          "source_id": "PM-218.4"
        }

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-buf-barrier"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-buf-soft",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding reference geometries, for every $\\eta>0$ there is a finite list of bounded piecewise regular profiles $h_1,\\ldots,h_N$, and a fine-mesh threshold, such that every inner observable $F_\\delta$ with $\\left\\|F_\\delta\\right\\|_\\infty\\le1$ has coefficients $c_1(\\delta),\\ldots,c_N(\\delta)$ satisfying $$\\sum_{i=1}^N|c_i(\\delta)|\\le1,\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{\\left\\|H_\\delta\\right\\|_\\infty\\le1}\n \\left|\\mathbb E[F_\\delta H_\\delta]\n       -\\sum_{i=1}^N c_i(\\delta)\n                    \\mathbb E[U_{\\delta,h_i}H_\\delta]\\right|<\\eta.\n \\label{buf:one-side}$$ The supremum is over all outer observables, including the constant one. The profiles and threshold are independent of $F_\\delta$ and $H_\\delta$. The coefficients may depend on the mesh and on the observable. The analogous statement holds with the inner and outer roles reversed.*\n\n*Inner replacement profiles can be supported within distance $Cs$ of the center; outer replacement profiles can be supported where $cR\\le |z|\\le CR$. The two profile regions have a fixed relative radial separation. Each profile is supported in a compact interior set avoiding the center, with additional positive clearances that may depend on $\\eta$.*\n\n\n*More precisely, let $(F_{\\alpha,\\delta})_{\\alpha=1}^p$ and $(H_{\\beta,\\delta})_{\\beta=1}^q$ be finite families of inner and outer observables, all of sup norm at most one. There are finite inner and outer profile lists $(h_i)$ and $(g_j)$ and coefficient vectors $c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta),d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\sum_i|c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta)|\\le1,\\qquad\n \\sum_j|d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)|\\le1,\\label{buf:matrix-mass}\\\\\n \\left|\\mathbb E[F_{\\alpha,\\delta}H_{\\beta,\\delta}]\n -\\sum_{i,j}c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta)d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)\n       \\mathbb E[U_{\\delta,h_i}U_{\\delta,g_j}]\\right|<\\eta\n \\quad\\text{for every }\\alpha,\\beta.\\label{buf:two-side}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The same row and column vectors work throughout this matrix. Adjoin the zero profile, for which $U_{\\delta,0}=1$, to each list. A constant observable can then be retained exactly; adjoining it to either family controls all the separate means with the same coefficients. The profile lists and mesh threshold are independent of the observables. The preceding support bounds apply to both lists. Parity and plane lattice-rotation symmetries may be imposed by averaging. The same conclusions hold along the approaching-half-plane charts described above.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-buf-soft",
          "exact_quote": "In the preceding reference geometries, for every $\\eta>0$ there is a finite list of bounded piecewise regular profiles $h_1,\\ldots,h_N$, and a fine-mesh threshold, such that every inner observable $F_\\delta$ with $\\left\\|F_\\delta\\right\\|_\\infty\\le1$ has coefficients $c_1(\\delta),\\ldots,c_N(\\delta)$ satisfying $$\\sum_{i=1}^N|c_i(\\delta)|\\le1,\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{\\left\\|H_\\delta\\right\\|_\\infty\\le1}\n \\left|\\mathbb E[F_\\delta H_\\delta]\n       -\\sum_{i=1}^N c_i(\\delta)\n                    \\mathbb E[U_{\\delta,h_i}H_\\delta]\\right|<\\eta.\n \\label{buf:one-side}$$ The supremum is over all outer observables, including the constant one. The profiles and threshold are independent of $F_\\delta$ and $H_\\delta$. The coefficients may depend on the mesh and on the observable. The analogous statement holds with the inner and outer roles reversed.*\n\n*Inner replacement profiles can be supported within distance $Cs$ of the center; outer replacement profiles can be supported where $cR\\le |z|\\le CR$. The two profile regions have a fixed relative radial separation. Each profile is supported in a compact interior set avoiding the center, with additional positive clearances that may depend on $\\eta$.*\n\n\n*More precisely, let $(F_{\\alpha,\\delta})_{\\alpha=1}^p$ and $(H_{\\beta,\\delta})_{\\beta=1}^q$ be finite families of inner and outer observables, all of sup norm at most one. There are finite inner and outer profile lists $(h_i)$ and $(g_j)$ and coefficient vectors $c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta),d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\sum_i|c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta)|\\le1,\\qquad\n \\sum_j|d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)|\\le1,\\label{buf:matrix-mass}\\\\\n \\left|\\mathbb E[F_{\\alpha,\\delta}H_{\\beta,\\delta}]\n -\\sum_{i,j}c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta)d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)\n       \\mathbb E[U_{\\delta,h_i}U_{\\delta,g_j}]\\right|<\\eta\n \\quad\\text{for every }\\alpha,\\beta.\\label{buf:two-side}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The same row and column vectors work throughout this matrix. Adjoin the zero profile, for which $U_{\\delta,0}=1$, to each list. A constant observable can then be retained exactly; adjoining it to either family controls all the separate means with the same coefficients. The profile lists and mesh threshold are independent of the observables. The preceding support bounds apply to both lists. Parity and plane lattice-rotation symmetries may be imposed by averaging. The same conclusions hold along the approaching-half-plane charts described above.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In the preceding reference geometries, for every $\\eta>0$ there is a finite list of bounded piecewise regular profiles $h_1,\\ldots,h_N$, and a fine-mesh threshold, such that every inner observable $F_\\delta$ with $\\left\\|F_\\delta\\right\\|_\\infty\\le1$ has coefficients $c_1(\\delta),\\ldots,c_N(\\delta)$ satisfying $$\\sum_{i=1}^N|c_i(\\delta)|\\le1,\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{\\left\\|H_\\delta\\right\\|_\\infty\\le1}\n \\left|\\mathbb E[F_\\delta H_\\delta]\n       -\\sum_{i=1}^N c_i(\\delta)\n                    \\mathbb E[U_{\\delta,h_i}H_\\delta]\\right|<\\eta.\n \\label{buf:one-side}$$ The supremum is over all outer observables, including the constant one. The profiles and threshold are independent of $F_\\delta$ and $H_\\delta$. The coefficients may depend on the mesh and on the observable. The analogous statement holds with the inner and outer roles reversed.*\n\n*Inner replacement profiles can be supported within distance $Cs$ of the center; outer replacement profiles can be supported where $cR\\le |z|\\le CR$. The two profile regions have a fixed relative radial separation. Each profile is supported in a compact interior set avoiding the center, with additional positive clearances that may depend on $\\eta$.*\n\n\n*More precisely, let $(F_{\\alpha,\\delta})_{\\alpha=1}^p$ and $(H_{\\beta,\\delta})_{\\beta=1}^q$ be finite families of inner and outer observables, all of sup norm at most one. There are finite inner and outer profile lists $(h_i)$ and $(g_j)$ and coefficient vectors $c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta),d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\sum_i|c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta)|\\le1,\\qquad\n \\sum_j|d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)|\\le1,\\label{buf:matrix-mass}\\\\\n \\left|\\mathbb E[F_{\\alpha,\\delta}H_{\\beta,\\delta}]\n -\\sum_{i,j}c_{\\alpha i}(\\delta)d_{\\beta j}(\\delta)\n       \\mathbb E[U_{\\delta,h_i}U_{\\delta,g_j}]\\right|<\\eta\n \\quad\\text{for every }\\alpha,\\beta.\\label{buf:two-side}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The same row and column vectors work throughout this matrix. Adjoin the zero profile, for which $U_{\\delta,0}=1$, to each list. A constant observable can then be retained exactly; adjoining it to either family controls all the separate means with the same coefficients. The profile lists and mesh threshold are independent of the observables. The preceding support bounds apply to both lists. Parity and plane lattice-rotation symmetries may be imposed by averaging. The same conclusions hold along the approaching-half-plane charts described above.",
      "explanation": "Any bounded inner observable is replaced, against all bounded outer tests, by a finite signed combination of soft weights with coefficient mass at most one.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-buf-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h_1,\\ldots,h_m$ be fixed bounded piecewise regular profiles with compact interior supports in a reference chart. All joint polynomial moments of the variables $X_{\\delta,h_i}$ converge to the integrals of the corresponding continuum spin correlations. For every $t<\\infty$, $$\\sup_{\\delta\\ \\mathrm{small}}\n \\mathbb E\\exp\\!\\left(t\\sum_{i=1}^m|X_{\\delta,h_i}|\\right)<\\infty.\n \\label{buf:exponential}$$ Joint exponential expectations converge as well. In particular the weights in <a href=\"#buf:weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"buf:weight\">[buf:weight]</a> have convergent normalizations and uniformly integrable powers of every fixed positive order.*\n\n\n*For two separated support regions with a fixed buffer, there is a geometric constant $K$ such that $$\\mathbb E[A U_{\\delta,g}]\\le K\\mathbb EA\n \\label{buf:weighted-tail}$$ for every nonnegative function $A$ of the first region and every normalized positive soft weight on the second. The constant is independent of $g$, its magnitude, and its complexity.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-rad-operator",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $C<\\infty$ and $\\ell_0<\\infty$ with the following property. Fix $\\ell=R/s\\geq\\ell_0$. For all sufficiently large $s$, with its threshold allowed to depend on $\\ell$, let $F$ be any bounded lattice function supported in $B_s(0)$. Put $$m(F)=\\mathbb E_{\\mathbb C}F,\n  \\qquad\n  d_R(F)=\\mathbb E^+_{\\mathrm{disk}(4R)}F-m(F).$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n |d_R(F)|&\\leq C(s/R)^d\\|F\\|_\\infty,\\label{eq:rad-response}\\\\\n \\|P_RF-m(F)\\|_\\infty\n   &\\leq C|d_R(F)|+C(s/R)^p\\|F\\|_\\infty,\n \\label{eq:rad-operator}\n\\end{aligned}$$ with $$(d,p)=\n \\begin{cases}\n (1,3),&F\\text{ even and invariant under right-angle rotations},\\\\\n (1,2),&F\\text{ even},\\\\\n (1/8,9/8),&F\\text{ odd}.\n \\end{cases}$$ The constants are independent of every sufficiently large fixed ratio.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-rad-spectrum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi_0$ be projection onto the vacuum. The following statements hold on the indicated closed sectors of $\\mathcal H$.*\n\n1.  *In the even plane sector there is a nonzero rank-one projection $\\Pi_1$ at dimension one and $$\\|D_t-\\Pi_0-t\\Pi_1\\|\\leq t^2.$$ On the subspace invariant under rotation by $\\pi/2$, the right side may be replaced by $t^3$.*\n\n2.  *In the odd plane sector there is a nonzero rank-one projection $\\Pi_{1/8}$ and $$\\|D_t-t^{1/8}\\Pi_{1/8}\\|\\leq t^{9/8}.$$*\n\n3.  *In the fixed half-plane, and in the even sector of the free half-plane, $$\\|D_t-\\Pi_0\\|\\leq t^2.$$*\n\n*The vacuum matrix element is $\\langle v,\\Pi_0w\\rangle\n=\\overline{\\mathbb Ev}\\,\\mathbb Ew$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rad-boundary",
        "exact_quote": "Fix support and separation factors, including a constant $C_0$. There are $C<\\infty$ and $\\ell_0<\\infty$ such that the following holds. For each fixed $\\ell=R/s\\geq\\ell_0$, there are a lower threshold for $s$, a sufficiently large chart-window factor, and a sufficiently small flatness threshold, all allowed to depend on $\\ell$ but not on the boundary orientation. Suppose a physical boundary point lies within $C_0s$ of the common center of the support and cutting squares, and that throughout the chart window the boundary is a single $C^1$ graph with slope deviation from its tangent below that threshold. Retain its homogeneous nearest-neighbor boundary type in the conditional averaging. Then, for every bounded $F$ supported in the inner square, $$\\inf_{c\\in\\mathbb R}\\|P_RF-c\\|_\\infty\n                       \\leq C(s/R)^2\\|F\\|_\\infty\n \\label{eq:rad-boundary-operator}$$ in fixed boundary conditions. The same holds for even $F$ in free boundary conditions. For complex $F$, take $c\\in\\mathbb C$ and adjust the constant harmlessly.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rad-boundary",
          "exact_quote": "Fix support and separation factors, including a constant $C_0$. There are $C<\\infty$ and $\\ell_0<\\infty$ such that the following holds. For each fixed $\\ell=R/s\\geq\\ell_0$, there are a lower threshold for $s$, a sufficiently large chart-window factor, and a sufficiently small flatness threshold, all allowed to depend on $\\ell$ but not on the boundary orientation. Suppose a physical boundary point lies within $C_0s$ of the common center of the support and cutting squares, and that throughout the chart window the boundary is a single $C^1$ graph with slope deviation from its tangent below that threshold. Retain its homogeneous nearest-neighbor boundary type in the conditional averaging. Then, for every bounded $F$ supported in the inner square, $$\\inf_{c\\in\\mathbb R}\\|P_RF-c\\|_\\infty\n                       \\leq C(s/R)^2\\|F\\|_\\infty\n \\label{eq:rad-boundary-operator}$$ in fixed boundary conditions. The same holds for even $F$ in free boundary conditions. For complex $F$, take $c\\in\\mathbb C$ and adjust the constant harmlessly.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix support and separation factors, including a constant $C_0$. There are $C<\\infty$ and $\\ell_0<\\infty$ such that the following holds. For each fixed $\\ell=R/s\\geq\\ell_0$, there are a lower threshold for $s$, a sufficiently large chart-window factor, and a sufficiently small flatness threshold, all allowed to depend on $\\ell$ but not on the boundary orientation. Suppose a physical boundary point lies within $C_0s$ of the common center of the support and cutting squares, and that throughout the chart window the boundary is a single $C^1$ graph with slope deviation from its tangent below that threshold. Retain its homogeneous nearest-neighbor boundary type in the conditional averaging. Then, for every bounded $F$ supported in the inner square, $$\\inf_{c\\in\\mathbb R}\\|P_RF-c\\|_\\infty\n                       \\leq C(s/R)^2\\|F\\|_\\infty\n \\label{eq:rad-boundary-operator}$$ in fixed boundary conditions. The same holds for even $F$ in free boundary conditions. For complex $F$, take $c\\in\\mathbb C$ and adjust the constant harmlessly.",
      "explanation": "Near a nearly flat physical boundary, conditional averaging has remainder of order the squared scale ratio.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
        "exact_quote": "The order of choices in the two propositions is important. The large ratio is fixed first; approximation tolerances smaller than its spectral remainder are then fixed, and only afterward is the lattice scale required to be sufficiently large. The geometric and spectral constants are uniform over large fixed ratios, but no uniform rate in that last fine-mesh passage has been asserted. For a fixed compact $C^2$ Jordan boundary, the graph and flatness conditions of Proposition <a href=\"#rad:boundary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rad:boundary\">16</a> hold on all sufficiently small physical windows, uniformly along the boundary.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rg-map",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed sufficiently large $L$, the maps $\\mathcal R_j$ are analytic on a common complex neighborhood of zero in <a href=\"#rg:norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rg:norm\">[rg:norm]</a>, uniformly in $j$ and the finite volume. They satisfy $\\mathcal R_j(0)=0$ and $$\\|\\mathcal R_j(h)-T_jh\\|_R\\le C_L\\|h\\|_r^2,\\qquad\n \\|D\\mathcal R_j(h)-T_j\\|\\le C_L\\|h\\|_r.\n \\label{rg:quadratic}$$ The derivative bound holds also from the marked norm at $r$ to the marked norm at $R$. For a marked coefficient, the corresponding scalar-increment functional is bounded, and its derivative differs from its derivative at zero by at most $C_L\\|h\\|_r$ in the same marked norms.*\n\n\n*The coefficient estimates remain valid if the output weight $e^{N_R(\\text{output carrier})}$ is replaced by $e^{2N_R(Q)}$. In particular, restricting an output list to connected test carriers with diameter at least $D$ gives the additional factor $$e^{-cD/R}.\n \\label{rg:diameter-gain}$$ This factor can be retained simultaneously with the norm of any small input, including a marked remainder or a background. The assertion holds before scalar projection as well as after it, and includes long unchanged singletons with their test neighborhoods.*\n\n*For any fixed number of square-free source labels, the induced maps on the corresponding coefficients are polynomial and uniformly Lipschitz on bounded sets of marked coefficients, while the background remains in this common small ball. All the preceding enhanced-weight and diameter bounds apply to these maps. Constants may depend on the number of labels and their norm bounds.*\n\n*In finite volume the transformation, including its scalar factor, preserves the partition integral exactly. The same conclusions apply to a preliminary transformation of finite-range microscopic interactions to a fixed large scale $r_0$, averaging every singleton there; its constants may also depend on $r_0$ and the finite microscopic range. Finitely many prescribed source carriers may instead be left unchanged if they are too large for these cutting squares, with constants allowed to depend on those carriers.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rg-linear",
        "exact_quote": "Given $\\rho>0$, choose $L$ sufficiently large and then $r_0$ sufficiently large. In the homogeneous background space there are uniformly bounded splittings into the line $\\mathbb R\\boldsymbol v_j$ and a complement such that $$T_j=\n \\begin{pmatrix}L&B_j\\\\0&D_j\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n \\|D_j\\|\\le\\rho,\\qquad \\|B_j\\|\\le C_L.\n \\label{rg:background-matrix}$$ The coordinates in this splitting are denoted by $(t_j,w_j)$.*\n\n\n*For a singleton marked source, in the even sector with $d=1$ or the odd sector with $d=1/8$, the scaled operator $L^dT_j$ has a corresponding splitting about $v_j^{(d)}$ with matrix $$\\begin{pmatrix}1&B_j^{(d)}\\\\0&D_j^{(d)}\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n \\|D_j^{(d)}\\|\\le\\rho,\\qquad \\|B_j^{(d)}\\|\\le C_L.\n \\label{rg:marked-matrix}$$ The even projections can be chosen invariant under square rotations.*\n\n*In a finite domain consider a boundary family: every carrier touches a fixed bounded lattice neighborhood of the physical edge. Its ordinary linear image, modulo constants, has norm at most $\\rho$ times its input norm, provided the local graph and flatness conditions of [rad:boundary](#rad:boundary) hold on the required windows at the current scale. For free boundary conditions the family must be even; for either fixed boundary condition no parity restriction is imposed. The image is again a boundary family.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rg-linear",
          "exact_quote": "Given $\\rho>0$, choose $L$ sufficiently large and then $r_0$ sufficiently large. Its ordinary linear image, modulo constants, has norm at most $\\rho$ times its input norm, provided the local graph and flatness conditions of [rad:boundary](#rad:boundary) hold on the required windows at the current scale.           "source_id": "PM-218.4"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flow-branch",
        "exact_quote": "There is $\\lambda_0>0$ and a real analytic branch $\\beta_c:(-\\lambda_0,\\lambda_0)\\to(0,\\infty)$, with $\\beta_c(0)=\\beta_0$, whose homogeneous effective backgrounds satisfy $$\\label{flow:plane-decay}\n       h_{j+1}=\\mathcal R_j(h_j),\\qquad\n       \\left\\|h_j\\right\\|_{r_j}\\le C|\\lambda|\\gamma_0^j.$$ The branch depends only on $J,V,\\lambda$.",

      "evidence": [
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **potential** ([source](#sec:model)): A potential assigns a real number $V(A)$ to each nonempty finite set $A\subset\mathbb Z^2$.
- **critical coupling** ([source](#model:spin)): Set $$K_c=\frac12\log(1+\sqrt2),\qquad \beta_0=K_c/J.$$
- **short carrier** ([source](#pm-h-23)): We call a carrier short if $N_r(A)\le M$.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-coupling | critical bond coupling K_c | = | $\frac12\log(1+\sqrt2)$ | fixed J |
| q-rate-ordering | contraction and decay parameters | < | $0<\rho<\gamma_0<\gamma<1$ | chosen after the contraction parameter |
| q-short-cutoff | short-carrier cutoff M | = | $M=\lceil(\log L)^2\rceil$ | large fixed L |
